│   ✅ Agent Finished                                                                                             │
│                                                                                                                 │
│   Message:                                                                                                      │
│     The implementation adds validation to the `ServerSentEvent` Pydantic model and the `format_sse_event`       │
│ utility function in `fastapi/sse.py` to ensure that the `id` and `event` fields do not contain newlines (`\n`   │
│ or `\r`), which would break the SSE wire protocol.                                                              │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     Specifically:                                                                                               │
│     - A helper function `_check_no_newlines` was introduced to validate that a string does not contain newline  │
│ characters.                                                                                                     │
│     - `ServerSentEvent.event` now uses `AfterValidator(_check_no_newlines)`.                                    │
│     - `ServerSentEvent.id` now uses `AfterValidator(_check_no_newlines)` in addition to the existing            │
│ `_check_id_no_null` validator.                                                                                  │
│     - `format_sse_event` now explicitly calls `_check_no_newlines(event)`, `_check_id_no_null(id)`, and         │
│ `_check_no_newlines(id)` at the start of the function to ensure all data is validated before being formatted    │
│ into the SSE wire format.                                                                                       │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     These changes prevent applications from sending malformed SSE streams that could disrupt client-side        │
│ parsing.                                                                                                        │

│   ✅ Agent Finished                                                                                             │

│   Message:                                                                                                      │
│     The issue was that header names containing underscores were still accepted by default when an explicit      │
│ alias was provided, violating the intended behavior where `convert_underscores=True` (the default) should treat │
│ underscores as hyphens.                                                                                         │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     I modified `fastapi/dependencies/utils.py` to ensure that the `replace("_", "-")` operation is applied to   │
│ the alias regardless of whether it was automatically generated from the field name or explicitly provided by    │
│ the user, both during dependency resolution in `solve_dependencies` and during parameter extraction in          │
│ `request_params_to_args`.                                                                                       │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     Specifically:                                                                                               │
│     1. In `solve_dependencies`, I updated the logic to convert the alias if `convert_underscores` is enabled,   │
│ ensuring the `field_info.alias` is updated to the hyphenated version.                                           │
│     2. In `request_params_to_args`, I updated the logic to ensure that when extracting headers, the alias used  │
│ for lookup is also converted to use hyphens if `convert_underscores` is enabled.                                │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     These changes ensure that the default behavior of converting underscores to hyphens is consistently applied │
│ across both the definition and the extraction of header parameters.                                             │

╭───────────────────────────────────────────────── Latest Detail ─────────────────────────────────────────────────╮
│   ✅ Agent Finished                                                                                             │
│                                                                                                                 │
│   Message:                                                                                                      │
│     The issue was that `requests` used `isinstance(obj, _SupportsRead)` to detect file-like objects. Since      │
│ `_SupportsRead` is a `runtime_checkable` Protocol, `isinstance` checks do not trigger `__getattr__`, causing    │
│ objects that delegate their `read` method via `__getattr__` (like certain wrappers) to be incorrectly           │
│ identified as not supporting read operations.                                                                   │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     I replaced `isinstance(obj, _SupportsRead)` with `hasattr(obj, 'read')` in `src/requests/models.py` in the  │
│ following locations:                                                                                            │
│     1. `_encode_params`: To correctly identify file-like objects passed as `data`.                              │
│     2. `_encode_files`: To ensure file-like objects in the `files` parameter are read before being passed to    │
│ the multipart encoder.                                                                                          │
│     3. `prepare_body`: To ensure the correct `content_type` is set when `data` is a file-like object.           │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     This ensures that wrappers using `__getattr__` for delegation are correctly recognized as supporting read   │
│ operations, maintaining consistency with the behavior of `hasattr` and ensuring correct handling of streamed    │
│ requests and file uploads.                                                                                      │

│     The issue was that `prepare_body` in `src/requests/models.py` failed to detect streams for objects that     │
│ provided an `__iter__` method via `__getattr__`, because `isinstance(data, Iterable)` returns `False` for such  │
│ objects.                                                                                                        │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     I have updated the stream detection logic to include `hasattr(data, "__iter__")` as a fallback.             │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     ```python                                                                                                   │
│     -        if isinstance(data, Iterable) and not isinstance(                                                  │
│     +        if (isinstance(data, Iterable) or hasattr(data, "__iter__")) and not isinstance(                   │
│                  data, (str, bytes, list, tuple, Mapping)                                                       │
│              ):                                                                                                 │
│     ```                                                                                                         │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     I verified the fix with a reproduction script that creates a wrapper providing `__iter__` via               │
│ `__getattr__`. Before the fix, this wrapper caused a `TypeError: 'getattr_wrapper' object is not iterable` in   │
│ `_encode_params`. After the fix, it is correctly identified as a stream, and the request is prepared with       │
│ `Transfer-Encoding: chunked`.                                                                                   │

╭───────────────────────────────────────────────── Latest Detail ─────────────────────────────────────────────────╮
│   ✅ Agent Finished                                                                                             │
│                                                                                                                 │
│   Message:                                                                                                      │
│     The issue was that `should_bypass_proxies` in `requests/utils.py` allowed incorrect substring matches when  │
│ checking if a host should bypass proxies based on the `no_proxy` configuration. For example, `example.com`      │
│ would match `myexample.com`.                                                                                    │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     I implemented a fix by:                                                                                     │
│     1.  Normalizing the `no_proxy` host by removing any leading dots.                                           │
│     2.  Ensuring that matches occur only if the hostname is exactly equal to the normalized host or if it ends  │
│ with `.` followed by the normalized host (ensuring it's a subdomain).                                           │
│     3.  Applying this logic to both hostnames and hostnames with ports.                                         │
│     4.  Updating the function to return `False` immediately if a `no_proxy` value was provided but no match was │
│ found, avoiding a fallback to the potentially buggy `urllib.request.proxy_bypass` in older Python versions.     │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     The fix was verified with a reproduction script and by running the existing test suite.                     │

╭───────────────────────────────────────────────── Latest Detail ─────────────────────────────────────────────────╮
│   ✅ Agent Finished                                                                                             │
│                                                                                                                 │
│   Message:                                                                                                      │
│     The bug where a `Response` object incorrectly included itself in its own `history` list during redirects    │
│ was caused by an incorrect slice `hist[1:]` in `Session.resolve_redirects`. This resulted in the current        │
│ response being added to its own history. The fix is to use `hist[:-1]`, ensuring that only previous responses   │
│ are included in the history.                                                                                    │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     The fix has been implemented in `src/requests/sessions.py`.                                                 │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     ```python                                                                                                   │
│                 # Update history and keep track of redirects.                                                   │
│                 # resp.history must ignore the original request in this loop                                    │
│                 hist.append(resp)                                                                               │
│     -            resp.history = hist[1:]                                                                        │
│     +            resp.history = hist[:-1]                                                                       │
│     ```                                                                                                         │

╭───────────────────────────────────────────────── Latest Detail ─────────────────────────────────────────────────╮
│   ✅ Agent Finished                                                                                             │
│                                                                                                                 │
│   Message:                                                                                                      │
│     The issue was that `FileProxy` inherited from `io.TextIOBase`, which provides a default `isatty()` method   │
│ that returns `False`. Because `isatty` was present in the base class, `FileProxy.__getattr__` was not called    │
│ when `isatty()` was accessed, causing `FileProxy` to always report `False` regardless of the underlying file's  │
│ status.                                                                                                         │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     I fixed this by explicitly overriding the `isatty` method in `FileProxy` to proxy the call to the           │
│ underlying file object, falling back to `False` if the underlying file does not implement `isatty`.             │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     Verified the fix with a reproduction script that confirmed `FileProxy.isatty()` now correctly returns the   │
│ value of the wrapped file's `isatty()` method.                                                                  │

│   ✅ Agent Finished                                                                                             │
│                                                                                                                 │
│   Message:                                                                                                      │
│     The bug where an `APIRouter` could be included into itself, causing an infinite loop and application hang,  │
│ has been fixed. A guard clause was added to `APIRouter.include_router` in `fastapi/routing.py` to check if the  │
│ router being included is the same instance as `self`. If so, a `FastAPIError` with the message `"APIRouter      │
│ cannot include itself"` is raised. This fix also covers `FastAPI.include_router` as it delegates to an internal │
│ `APIRouter` instance. The fix was verified with a reproduction script.                                          │

╭───────────────────────────────────────────────── Latest Detail ─────────────────────────────────────────────────╮
│   ✅ Agent Finished                                                                                             │
│                                                                                                                 │
│   Message:                                                                                                      │
│     The bug was caused by `APIRouter` assigning `on_startup` and `on_shutdown` handlers to `self` before        │
│ calling `super().__init__()`. Since `APIRouter` inherits from Starlette's `Router`, the parent class's          │
│ initialization was overwriting these attributes, causing the handlers to be lost.                               │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     I have moved the assignment of `self.on_startup` and `self.on_shutdown` to occur after the                  │
│ `super().__init__()` call in `fastapi/routing.py`. This ensures that the user-provided handlers are preserved   │
│ and correctly used by the `_DefaultLifespan` context manager during the application's startup and shutdown      │
│ phases.                                                                                                         │

│ forbade the use of `Depends` for special types like `Request`, `Response`, `WebSocket`, and others. This        │
│ prevented users from using dependencies to override or provide these special types.                             │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     The fix modifies the logic in `analyze_param` to skip this assertion if `Depends` is present, allowing the  │
│ dependency to be processed normally. This change also extends the same capability to other special types such   │
│ as `Request`, `WebSocket`, and `HTTPConnection`.                                                                │
│                                                                                                                 │
│     The fix was verified with a reproduction script and a comprehensive test suite in                           │
│ `tests/test_response_dependency.py` covering both `Annotated[Response, Depends(...)]` and `Response =           │
│ Depends(...)` patterns.                                                                                         │
